This contract governs PROSE DELIVERABLES ONLY: documents, README/markdown prose, CVs, cover letters, emails, reports, essays, articles, and any drafted text the user will publish or send. It NEVER touches: source code, code comments, commit messages, JSON/YAML/config, CLI output, logs, error messages, or the conversational register of chat itself.\nComposes with other active modes; it does not override them. A chat-compression mode (such as caveman) owns how you talk in chat - keep chat terse if it is on; this contract only shapes the deliverable you write, not the chat around it. A code-simplicity mode (such as ponytail) owns code - this contract never touches code, so there is nothing to conflict. Each mode keeps its own domain: terse chat, lazy code, human prose. When drafting deliverable text inside a chat reply, these rules apply to the draft, not to the surrounding chat.\n\nRules for prose:\n- Vary sentence length irregularly: a short sentence, then a long one that develops it. Never metronomic, never mechanical short-long alternation.\n- Banned vocabulary (use plain alternatives): delve, tapestry, pivotal, crucial, leverage, robust, seamless, foster, underscore, showcase, landscape (abstract), journey (abstract), realm, multifaceted, holistic, testament, vibrant, comprehensive, plethora, myriad, boast, elevate, empower, unlock, game-changer, supercharge, genuinely, fascinating, nuanced.\n- Banned moves: rule-of-three flourishes; \"it's not just X, it's Y\"; hedge stacking (two hedges in one sentence); signposting (\"let's dive in\"); empty pivots (\"it's worth noting\"); \"In conclusion / Overall\" closers; outcome-speculation tails (\", paving the way for\"); self-thoroughness (\"this comprehensive guide\"); generic upbeat endings; chatbot phrases (\"I hope this helps\").\n- Em-dash: at most one per paragraph. No bold-for-emphasis inside prose sentences. No emojis in prose. Semicolons and parentheses where a writer would naturally use them.\n- Mode: factual/encyclopedic content stays neutral third-person - never inject first-person voice or opinions into it. First-person/opinion content: contract naturally (it's, don't), take real stances.\n- Preserve exactly: numbers, units, dates, proper nouns, citations, quotes, technical terms. Never invent facts, sources, or statistics.\n- Concrete subjects, active verbs. End sections on a fact or observation, not a sentiment.\n- SILENT. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Pass the script inline via `script` — do not Write it to a file first, and do not also set the tool's `name` input (that selects a saved workflow); it is plain JavaScript, not TypeScript.\n\nThe canonical multi-stage pattern — pipeline by default, each dimension verifies as soon as its review completes:\n  export const meta = {\n    name: 'review-changes',\n    description: 'Review changed files across dimensions, verify each finding',\n    phases: [{ title: 'Review' }, { title: 'Verify' }],\n  }\n  const DIMENSIONS = [{key: 'bugs', prompt: '...'}, {key: 'perf', prompt: '...'}]\n  const results = await pipeline(\n    DIMENSIONS,\n    d => agent(d.prompt, {label: `review:${d.key}`, phase: 'Review', schema: FINDINGS_SCHEMA}),\n    review => parallel(review.findings.map(f => () =>\n      agent(`Adversarially verify: ${f.title}`, {label: `verify:${f.file}`, phase: 'Verify', schema: VERDICT_SCHEMA})\n        .then(v => ({...f, verdict: v}))\n    ))\n  )\n  const confirmed = results.flat().filter(Boolean).filter(f => f.verdict?.isReal)\n  return { confirmed }\n  // Dimension 'bugs' findings verify while dimension 'perf' is still reviewing. No wasted wall-clock.\n\nBefore writing a script, load the `workflow-authoring` skill — the workflow authoring reference: script API and gotchas, resume, the **Ultracode** section, quality patterns, worked examples.\n\nThis session has the default workflow size guideline: medium — keep workflows under 15 agents. This is a guideline, not a hard limit — follow it unless the user's prompt calls for a different scale. The user can raise or remove it with \"Dynamic workflow size\" in /config."
````text
    },
    {
      "name": "Write",
````

```text
1	// Pure helpers for sidebar activity classification. No React imports.
2	// Used by useHomeViewModel to build the Active tab dataset.
3	
4	import { isTerminalRunStatus, normalizeRunStatus } from "@/lib/run-status";
5	import { isWorkerActiveStatus } from "@/lib/conversation-workers";
6	import { isRunUnread, resolveRunLatestUnreadTimestamp } from "@/lib/conversation-state";
7	import type { SidebarGroup, SidebarRun } from "./types";
8	
9	export const ACTIVE_SESSION_ACTIVITY_WINDOW_MS = 20 * 60 * 1000;
10	
11	function parseTimestampMs(value: string | null | undefined): number {
12	  if (!value) return 0;
13	  const t = new Date(value).getTime();
14	  return Number.isFinite(t) ? t : 0;
15	}
16	
17	function laterOf(a: string | null | undefined, b: string | null | undefined): string | null {
18	  const aMs = parseTimestampMs(a);
19	  const bMs = parseTimestampMs(b);
20	  if (!a && !b) return null;
21	  return aMs >= bMs ? (a ?? null) : (b ?? null);
22	}
23	
24	function maxTimestamp(...values: (string | null | undefined)[]): string | null {
25	  return values.reduce<string | null>((acc, v) => laterOf(acc, v), null);
26	}
27	
28	// User-authored input rows only — excludes supervisor, internal, checkpoint, intervention
29	function isUserInputMessage(message: { role: string; kind?: string | null }): boolean {
30	  if ((message.role ?? "").toLowerCase() !== "user") return false;
31	  const kind = (message.kind ?? "").toLowerCase();
32	  return kind !== "checkpoint" && kind !== "internal" && kind !== "intervention";
33	}
34	
35	type RunInput = {
36	  id: string;
37	  status: string;
38	  createdAt: string;
39	  updatedAt?: string | null;
40	  lastActivityAt?: string | null;
41	};
42	
43	type WorkerInput = {
44	  id: string;
45	  runId: string;
46	  status: string;
47	  updatedAt?: string;
48	};
49	
50	type AgentInput = {
51	  name: string; // workerId
52	  state: string;
53	  updatedAt?: string;
54	  lastText?: string;
55	  currentText?: string;
56	  displayText?: string;
57	};
58	
59	type MessageInput = {
60	  runId: string;
61	  role: string;
62	  kind?: string | null;
63	  createdAt: string;
64	};
65	
66	type QueuedMessageInput = {
67	  runId: string;
68	  createdAt: string;
69	  updatedAt: string;
70	  deliveredAt?: string | null;
71	};
72	
73	/**
74	 * Per-run rollups computed in ONE pass over each global collection.
75	 *
76	 * Without this, classifying N runs cost N full scans of every message, queued
77	 * message, worker and agent in the app — and the sidebar did that three times
78	 * over on every SSE frame, allocating two `Date` objects per message compared.
79	 * With ~40 runs and a few thousand accumulated messages that is hundreds of
80	 * thousands of iterations per frame, which is what made the sidebar the single
81	 * most expensive thing on the client's main thread while sessions streamed.
82	 *
83	 * Build once with `buildSidebarActivityIndex`, reuse for every run.
84	 */
85	export interface SidebarActivityIndex {
86	  latestMessageAtByRunId: Map<string, string>;
87	  latestUserInputAtByRunId: Map<string, string>;
88	  workersByRunId: Map<string, WorkerInput[]>;
89	  agentsByWorkerId: Map<string, AgentInput>;
90	}
91	
92	export function buildSidebarActivityIndex(args: {
93	  messages: MessageInput[];
94	  queuedMessages: QueuedMessageInput[];
95	  workers: WorkerInput[];
96	  agents: AgentInput[];
97	}): SidebarActivityIndex {
98	  const latestMessageAtByRunId = new Map<string, string>();
99	  const latestUserInputAtByRunId = new Map<string, string>();
100	  const workersByRunId = new Map<string, WorkerInput[]>();
101	  const agentsByWorkerId = new Map<string, AgentInput>();
102	
103	  const keepLater = (map: Map<string, string>, runId: string, value: string | null | undefined) => {
104	    if (!value) return;
105	    const existing = map.get(runId);
106	    if (!existing || parseTimestampMs(value) > parseTimestampMs(existing)) {
107	      map.set(runId, value);
108	    }
109	  };
110	
111	  for (const message of args.messages) {
112	    keepLater(latestMessageAtByRunId, message.runId, message.createdAt);
113	    if (isUserInputMessage(message)) {
114	      keepLater(latestUserInputAtByRunId, message.runId, message.createdAt);
115	    }
116	  }
117	
118	  for (const queued of args.queuedMessages) {
119	    keepLater(
120	      latestUserInputAtByRunId,
121	      queued.runId,
122	      queued.deliveredAt ?? queued.updatedAt ?? queued.createdAt,
123	    );
124	  }
125	
126	  for (const worker of args.workers) {
127	    const bucket = workersByRunId.get(worker.runId);
128	    if (bucket) {
129	      bucket.push(worker);
130	    } else {
131	      workersByRunId.set(worker.runId, [worker]);
132	    }
133	  }
134	
135	  for (const agent of args.agents) {
136	    agentsByWorkerId.set(agent.name, agent);
137	  }
138	
139	  return { latestMessageAtByRunId, latestUserInputAtByRunId, workersByRunId, agentsByWorkerId };
140	}
141	
142	function indexFromArgs(args: {
143	  messages: MessageInput[];
144	  queuedMessages: QueuedMessageInput[];
145	  workers: WorkerInput[];
146	  agents: AgentInput[];
147	}) {
148	  return buildSidebarActivityIndex(args);
149	}
150	
151	/**
152	 * Indexed twin of `getRunLatestUnreadTimestamp` from `@/lib/conversation-state`.
153	 * Same status rules (shared implementation), no per-run message scan.
154	 */
155	export function getIndexedRunLatestUnreadTimestamp(
156	  run: RunInput,
157	  index: SidebarActivityIndex,
158	): string | null {
159	  return resolveRunLatestUnreadTimestamp(run, index.latestMessageAtByRunId.get(run.id) ?? null);
160	}
161	
162	export interface SidebarRunActivityArgs {
163	  run: RunInput;
164	  messages: MessageInput[];
165	  readMarkers: Record<string, string>;
166	  workers: WorkerInput[];
167	  agents: AgentInput[];
168	  queuedMessages: QueuedMessageInput[];
169	  workerOutputObservedAtByRunId: Record<string, string>;
170	  nowMs: number;
171	  selectedRunId?: string | null;
172	}
173	
174	// recentActivityAt: max of eligible user-input and worker-output signals
175	export function getSidebarRunLastActivityAt(args: SidebarRunActivityArgs): string | null {
176	  return getIndexedSidebarRunLastActivityAt(args, indexFromArgs(args));
177	}
178	
179	function getIndexedSidebarRunLastActivityAt(
180	  args: Pick<SidebarRunActivityArgs, "run" | "workerOutputObservedAtByRunId">,
181	  index: SidebarActivityIndex,
182	): string | null {
183	  const userInputAt = index.latestUserInputAtByRunId.get(args.run.id) ?? null;
184	  const workerOutputAt = args.workerOutputObservedAtByRunId[args.run.id] ?? null;
185	  return maxTimestamp(userInputAt, workerOutputAt);
186	}
187	
188	function getIndexedWorkingActivityAt(run: RunInput, index: SidebarActivityIndex): string | null {
189	  const runWorkers = index.workersByRunId.get(run.id) ?? [];
190	  const timestamps: (string | null | undefined)[] = [run.updatedAt];
191	
192	  for (const worker of runWorkers) {
193	    if (isWorkerActiveStatus(worker.status)) {
194	      timestamps.push(worker.updatedAt);
195	    }
196	    const agent = index.agentsByWorkerId.get(worker.id);
197	    if (agent && isWorkerActiveStatus(agent.state)) {
198	      timestamps.push(agent.updatedAt);
199	    }
200	  }
201	
202	  timestamps.push(run.createdAt);
203	  return maxTimestamp(...timestamps);
204	}
205	
206	export function isSidebarRunCurrentlyWorking(
207	  args: Pick<SidebarRunActivityArgs, "run" | "workers" | "agents">,
208	): boolean {
209	  return isIndexedSidebarRunCurrentlyWorking(args.run, buildSidebarActivityIndex({
210	    messages: [],
211	    queuedMessages: [],
212	    workers: args.workers,
213	    agents: args.agents,
214	  }));
215	}
216	
217	function isIndexedSidebarRunCurrentlyWorking(run: RunInput, index: SidebarActivityIndex): boolean {
218	  // Terminal runs are never "working" — stale worker metadata loses.
219	  if (isTerminalRunStatus(run.status)) return false;
220	
221	  const normalizedStatus = normalizeRunStatus(run.status);
222	  if (normalizedStatus === "needs_recovery") return false;
223	
224	  if (normalizedStatus !== "running" && normalizedStatus !== "awaiting_user") return false;
225	
226	  const runWorkers = index.workersByRunId.get(run.id) ?? [];
227	
228	  for (const worker of runWorkers) {
229	    if (isWorkerActiveStatus(worker.status)) return true;
230	    const agent = index.agentsByWorkerId.get(worker.id);
231	    if (agent && isWorkerActiveStatus(agent.state)) return true;
232	  }
233	
234	  // Run is "running" with no active workers/agents yet — still counts.
235	  return normalizedStatus === "running";
236	}
237	
238	export interface RunActiveClassification {
239	  isActive: boolean;
240	  isUnread: boolean;
241	  isWorking: boolean;
242	  isRecent: boolean;
243	  recentActivityAt: string | null;
244	  activeSortAt: string | null;
245	}
246	
247	export function classifySidebarRun(args: SidebarRunActivityArgs): RunActiveClassification {
248	  return classifyIndexedSidebarRun(args, indexFromArgs(args));
249	}
250	
251	/**
252	 * The hot path. `buildActiveConversationGroups` builds the index once and calls
253	 * this per run, so classification is O(workers in run) instead of O(all
254	 * messages + all workers + all agents).
255	 */
256	export function classifyIndexedSidebarRun(
257	  args: Pick<SidebarRunActivityArgs, "run" | "readMarkers" | "workerOutputObservedAtByRunId" | "nowMs" | "selectedRunId">,
258	  index: SidebarActivityIndex,
259	): RunActiveClassification {
260	  const { run, readMarkers, nowMs } = args;
261	  const lastReadAt = readMarkers[run.id] ?? null;
262	  const normalizedStatus = normalizeRunStatus(run.status);
263	
264	  const latestUnreadAt = getIndexedRunLatestUnreadTimestamp(run, index);
265	  const isUnread = isRunUnread({ latestMessageAt: latestUnreadAt, lastReadAt });
266	
267	  const isWorking = isIndexedSidebarRunCurrentlyWorking(run, index);
268	
269	  const recentActivityAt = getIndexedSidebarRunLastActivityAt(args, index);
270	  const allowsRecentActivity = normalizedStatus !== "cancelled" && normalizedStatus !== "canceled";
271	  const isRecent =
272	    allowsRecentActivity &&
273	    recentActivityAt !== null &&
274	    nowMs - parseTimestampMs(recentActivityAt) <= ACTIVE_SESSION_ACTIVITY_WINDOW_MS;
275	
276	  const isSelected = args.selectedRunId != null && args.run.id === args.selectedRunId;
277	  const isActive = isUnread || isWorking || isRecent || isSelected;
278	
279	  let activeSortAt: string | null = recentActivityAt;
280	  if (isUnread && latestUnreadAt) {
281	    activeSortAt = maxTimestamp(activeSortAt, latestUnreadAt);
282	  }
283	  if (isWorking) {
284	    activeSortAt = maxTimestamp(activeSortAt, getIndexedWorkingActivityAt(run, index));
285	  }
286	  // Worker output is only observed live (see SidebarWorkerActivityManager), so a
287	  // run whose last turn finished before this page load has no in-memory signal.
288	  // The persisted activity stamp covers that gap; creation date is the last resort.
289	  if (!activeSortAt) activeSortAt = run.lastActivityAt ?? run.createdAt;
290	
291	  return { isActive, isUnread, isWorking, isRecent, recentActivityAt, activeSortAt };
292	}
293	
294	export function compareActiveSidebarRunsDesc(
295	  a: { activeSortAt: string | null; createdAt: string; id: string },
296	  b: { activeSortAt: string | null; createdAt: string; id: string },
297	): number {
298	  const diff = parseTimestampMs(b.activeSortAt) - parseTimestampMs(a.activeSortAt);
299	  if (diff !== 0) return diff;
300	  const createdDiff = parseTimestampMs(b.createdAt) - parseTimestampMs(a.createdAt);
301	  if (createdDiff !== 0) return createdDiff;
302	  return a.id < b.id ? -1 : a.id > b.id ? 1 : 0;
303	}
304	
305	export interface BuildActiveGroupsArgs {
306	  groups: SidebarGroup[];
307	  messages: MessageInput[];
308	  readMarkers: Record<string, string>;
309	  workers: WorkerInput[];
310	  agents: AgentInput[];
311	  queuedMessages: QueuedMessageInput[];
312	  workerOutputObservedAtByRunId: Record<string, string>;
313	  nowMs: number;
314	  selectedRunId?: string | null;
315	}
316	
317	export function buildActiveConversationGroups(args: BuildActiveGroupsArgs): SidebarGroup[] {
318	  const { groups, messages, readMarkers, workers, agents, queuedMessages, workerOutputObservedAtByRunId, nowMs, selectedRunId } = args;
319	
320	  const activeGroups: Array<{ group: SidebarGroup; latestActivityMs: number }> = [];
321	
322	  // One pass over the global collections, reused for every run below. This
323	  // replaces N full scans of messages/queued/workers/agents per frame.
324	  const index = buildSidebarActivityIndex({ messages, queuedMessages, workers, agents });
325	
326	  for (const group of groups) {
327	    const activeRuns: Array<SidebarRun & { activeSortAt: string | null }> = [];
328	    let groupLatestMs = 0;
329	
330	    for (const run of group.runs) {
331	      const result = classifyIndexedSidebarRun({
332	        run: {
333	          id: run.id,
334	          status: run.status,
335	          createdAt: run.createdAt,
336	          updatedAt: (run as SidebarRun & { updatedAt?: string | null }).updatedAt ?? null,
337	          lastActivityAt: run.lastActivityAt ?? null,
338	        },
339	        readMarkers,
340	        workerOutputObservedAtByRunId,
341	        nowMs,
342	        selectedRunId,
343	      }, index);
344	
345	      if (!result.isActive) continue;
346	
347	      activeRuns.push({ ...run, activeSortAt: result.activeSortAt });
348	      const ms = parseTimestampMs(result.activeSortAt);
349	      if (ms > groupLatestMs) groupLatestMs = ms;
350	    }
351	
352	    if (activeRuns.length === 0) continue;
353	
354	    activeRuns.sort(compareActiveSidebarRunsDesc);
355	
356	    activeGroups.push({
357	      group: { path: group.path, name: group.name, runs: activeRuns },
358	      latestActivityMs: groupLatestMs,
359	    });
360	  }
361	
362	  activeGroups.sort((a, b) => {
363	    const diff = b.latestActivityMs - a.latestActivityMs;
364	    if (diff !== 0) return diff;
365	    if (a.group.name < b.group.name) return -1;
366	    if (a.group.name > b.group.name) return 1;
367	    if (a.group.path < b.group.path) return -1;
368	    if (a.group.path > b.group.path) return 1;
369	    return 0;
370	  });
371	
372	  return activeGroups.map((entry) => entry.group);
373	}
374	
375	// Apply search to the active groups.
376	// If the query matches the project name, all active sessions in that project remain visible.
377	// Otherwise filter active sessions whose title or path matches the query.
378	export function filterActiveConversationGroups(
379	  activeGroups: SidebarGroup[],
380	  searchQuery: string,
381	): SidebarGroup[] {
382	  if (!searchQuery) return activeGroups;
383	  const q = searchQuery.toLowerCase();
384	
385	  return activeGroups
386	    .map((group) => {
387	      if (group.name.toLowerCase().includes(q)) {
388	        // Project name matches — show all active sessions in this project.
389	        return group;
390	      }
391	      // Otherwise filter sessions by title or path.
392	      const runs = group.runs.filter(
393	        (run) => run.title.toLowerCase().includes(q) || run.path.toLowerCase().includes(q),
394	      );
395	      return { ...group, runs };
396	    })
397	    .filter((group) => group.runs.length > 0);
398	}
```

```text
1	"use client";
2	
3	import { useRef } from "react";
4	import type { SidebarGroup } from "./types";
5	
6	// The Recent ("Active") tab lists conversations sorted by latest activity. That
7	// membership and sort are recomputed on every event-stream update, so while the
8	// user watches the tab rows can disappear, appear, or reshuffle on every turn.
9	// This hook freezes the row/group membership and order captured at the moment
10	// the tab becomes active, then overlays any still-present live row data so
11	// statuses and unread indicators can update without changing the list itself.
12	// Leaving the tab and returning re-snapshots the list.
13	
14	type SnapshottedRun = SidebarGroup["runs"][number];
15	
16	type SnapshottedGroup = {
17	  group: SidebarGroup;
18	  runOrder: string[];
19	  runsById: Map<string, SnapshottedRun>;
20	};
21	
22	type OrderSnapshot = {
23	  groupOrder: string[];
24	  groupsByPath: Map<string, SnapshottedGroup>;
25	};
26	
27	function captureOrder(groups: SidebarGroup[]): OrderSnapshot {
28	  const groupOrder: string[] = [];
29	  const groupsByPath = new Map<string, SnapshottedGroup>();
30	
31	  groups.forEach((group) => {
32	    groupOrder.push(group.path);
33	    const runOrder = group.runs.map((run) => run.id);
34	    const runsById = new Map(group.runs.map((run) => [run.id, run]));
35	    groupsByPath.set(group.path, {
36	      group: { ...group, runs: [...group.runs] },
37	      runOrder,
38	      runsById,
39	    });
40	  });
41	  return { groupOrder, groupsByPath };
42	}
43	
44	function applyOrder(groups: SidebarGroup[], snapshot: OrderSnapshot, liveCatalogGroups: SidebarGroup[] = groups): SidebarGroup[] {
45	  const liveGroupsByPath = new Map(groups.map((group) => [group.path, group]));
46	  const liveCatalogGroupsByPath = new Map(liveCatalogGroups.map((group) => [group.path, group]));
47	
48	  return snapshot.groupOrder.flatMap((groupPath) => {
49	    const snapshotted = snapshot.groupsByPath.get(groupPath);
50	    if (!snapshotted) return [];
51	
52	    const liveGroup = liveGroupsByPath.get(groupPath);
53	    const liveCatalogGroup = liveCatalogGroupsByPath.get(groupPath);
54	    const liveRunsById = new Map((liveGroup?.runs ?? []).map((run) => [run.id, run]));
55	    const liveCatalogRunsById = new Map((liveCatalogGroup?.runs ?? []).map((run) => [run.id, run]));
56	    const runs = snapshotted.runOrder.flatMap((runId) => {
57	      const run = liveRunsById.get(runId) ?? liveCatalogRunsById.get(runId) ?? snapshotted.runsById.get(runId);
58	      return run ? [run] : [];
59	    });
60	
61	    if (runs.length === 0) return [];
62	
63	    return [{
64	      ...(liveGroup ?? liveCatalogGroup ?? snapshotted.group),
65	      runs,
66	    }];
67	  });
68	}
69	
70	export function useFrozenRecentOrder(
71	  activeProjects: SidebarGroup[],
72	  isRecentTabActive: boolean,
73	  liveCatalogProjects: SidebarGroup[] = activeProjects,
74	): SidebarGroup[] {
75	  const snapshotRef = useRef<OrderSnapshot | null>(null);
76	  const wasActiveRef = useRef(false);
77	
78	  if (!isRecentTabActive) {
79	    // Tab hidden — drop the snapshot so re-entry sorts fresh by latest activity.
80	    snapshotRef.current = null;
81	    wasActiveRef.current = false;
82	    return activeProjects;
83	  }
84	
85	  if (!wasActiveRef.current || snapshotRef.current === null) {
86	    // Just (re)opened the tab — freeze the current latest-activity order.
87	    snapshotRef.current = captureOrder(activeProjects);
88	    wasActiveRef.current = true;
89	    return applyOrder(activeProjects, snapshotRef.current, liveCatalogProjects);
90	  }
91	
92	  return applyOrder(activeProjects, snapshotRef.current, liveCatalogProjects);
93	}
94
```

```text
1	import { t } from "@/lib/i18n";
2	import { normalizeExplicitProjectPaths, normalizeProjectPath, resolveStaleProjectFallback, resolveStoredProjectRoot } from "@/lib/project-paths";
3	
4	type PlanRecord = { id: string; path: string };
5	type RunRecord = {
6	  id: string;
7	  planId: string;
8	  mode?: string | null;
9	  status: string;
10	  createdAt: string;
11	  updatedAt?: string | null;
12	  lastActivityAt?: string | null;
13	  projectPath?: string | null;
14	  title?: string | null;
15	  preferredWorkerType?: string | null;
16	};
17	
18	export type ConversationGroup = {
19	  path: string;
20	  name: string;
21	  runs: Array<{
22	    id: string;
23	    title: string;
24	    path: string;
25	    mode?: string | null;
26	    status: string;
27	    createdAt: string;
28	    updatedAt?: string | null;
29	    lastActivityAt?: string | null;
30	    preferredWorkerType?: string | null;
31	  }>;
32	};
33	
34	function timestampMs(value: string | null | undefined) {
35	  if (!value) return 0;
36	  const parsed = new Date(value).getTime();
37	  return Number.isFinite(parsed) ? parsed : 0;
38	}
39	
40	/**
41	 * Order sessions by conversation activity, newest first.
42	 *
43	 * `lastActivityAt` is written server-side on the last user message and the last
44	 * finished agent turn (see the `runs_activity_*` triggers in
45	 * `src/server/db/index.ts`). Sorting here rather than relying on the snapshot's
46	 * `ORDER BY` matters because `EventStreamStateManager` merges runs by id and
47	 * appends unseen ones to the end — an optimistically created run would
48	 * otherwise sink to the bottom of its project until the next full snapshot.
49	 */
50	function compareConversationRunsDesc(
51	  a: { lastActivityAt?: string | null; createdAt: string; id: string },
52	  b: { lastActivityAt?: string | null; createdAt: string; id: string },
53	) {
54	  const activityDiff =
55	    Math.max(timestampMs(b.lastActivityAt), timestampMs(b.createdAt))
56	    - Math.max(timestampMs(a.lastActivityAt), timestampMs(a.createdAt));
57	  if (activityDiff !== 0) return activityDiff;
58	  const createdDiff = timestampMs(b.createdAt) - timestampMs(a.createdAt);
59	  if (createdDiff !== 0) return createdDiff;
60	  return a.id < b.id ? -1 : a.id > b.id ? 1 : 0;
61	}
62	
63	function findMatchingProject(planPath: string, explicitProjects: string[]) {
64	  return (
65	    explicitProjects.find((projectPath) => {
66	      const folderName = projectPath.split("/").pop() || projectPath;
67	      return planPath.startsWith(projectPath) || planPath.includes(folderName);
68	    }) ?? null
69	  );
70	}
71	
72	export function buildConversationGroups(args: {
73	  explicitProjects: string[];
74	  plans: PlanRecord[];
75	  runs: RunRecord[];
76	}) {
77	  const staleFallbackProject = resolveStaleProjectFallback(
78	    args.explicitProjects,
79	    args.runs.map((run) => run.projectPath),
80	  );
81	  const mappedRuns = args.runs
82	    .map((run) => {
83	      const plan = args.plans.find((candidate) => candidate.id === run.planId);
84	      const planPath = plan?.path ?? run.projectPath ?? run.id;
85	
86	      const projectPath = resolveStoredProjectRoot(run.projectPath, args.explicitProjects, { staleFallbackProject })
87	        ?? (plan ? findMatchingProject(plan.path, args.explicitProjects) : null);
88	      return {
89	        id: run.id,
90	        groupPath: projectPath ? normalizeProjectPath(projectPath) : "other",
91	        title: run.title || "New conversation",
92	        path: planPath,
93	        mode: run.mode,
94	        status: run.status,
95	        createdAt: run.createdAt,
96	        updatedAt: run.updatedAt ?? null,
97	        lastActivityAt: run.lastActivityAt ?? null,
98	        preferredWorkerType: run.preferredWorkerType,
99	      };
100	    })
101	    .filter((run): run is NonNullable<typeof run> => Boolean(run))
102	    .sort(compareConversationRunsDesc);
103	
104	  const groups = new Map<string, ConversationGroup>();
105	
106	  for (const normalizedPath of normalizeExplicitProjectPaths(args.explicitProjects)) {
107	    groups.set(normalizedPath, {
108	      path: normalizedPath,
109	      name: normalizedPath.split("/").pop() || normalizedPath,
110	      runs: [],
111	    });
112	  }
113	
114	  for (const run of mappedRuns) {
115	    if (groups.has(run.groupPath)) {
116	      continue;
117	    }
118	
119	    if (run.groupPath === "other") {
120	      groups.set(run.groupPath, {
121	        path: run.groupPath,
122	        name: t("conversation.sidebar.otherSessions"),
123	        runs: [],
124	      });
125	      continue;
126	    }
127	
128	    groups.set(run.groupPath, {
129	      path: run.groupPath,
130	      name: run.groupPath.split("/").pop() || run.groupPath,
131	      runs: [],
132	    });
133	  }
134	
135	  const explicitGroups = Array.from(groups.values()).map((group) => ({
136	    ...group,
137	    runs: mappedRuns
138	      .filter((run) => run.groupPath === group.path)
139	      .map((run) => ({
140	        id: run.id,
141	        title: run.title,
142	        path: run.path,
143	        mode: run.mode,
144	        status: run.status,
145	        createdAt: run.createdAt,
146	        updatedAt: run.updatedAt,
147	        lastActivityAt: run.lastActivityAt,
148	        preferredWorkerType: run.preferredWorkerType,
149	      })),
150	  }));
151	
152	  const otherRuns = mappedRuns
153	    .filter((run) => run.groupPath === "other")
154	    .map((run) => ({
155	      id: run.id,
156	      title: run.title,
157	      path: run.path,
158	      mode: run.mode,
159	      status: run.status,
160	      createdAt: run.createdAt,
161	      updatedAt: run.updatedAt,
162	      lastActivityAt: run.lastActivityAt,
163	      preferredWorkerType: run.preferredWorkerType,
164	    }));
165	
166	  if (otherRuns.length > 0 && !groups.has("other")) {
167	    explicitGroups.push({
168	      path: "other",
169	      name: t("conversation.sidebar.otherSessions"),
170	      runs: otherRuns,
171	    });
172	  }
173	
174	  return explicitGroups;
175	}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:36:40.948Z

The sort key is deterministic based on the max of lastActivityAt and createdAt, so the oscillation likely stems from partial updates overwriting the run record without lastActivityAt, followed by a full snapshot restoring it — causing the sort key to flip between the two timestamps and the session to jump position.

This matches the "surrounded by 2 others" symptom. I should check how EventStreamStateManager merges run updates, and whether partial updates drop lastActivityAt — though the modified files in git status (LiveEventConnectionManager, RunnerConnection, useHomeLifecycle) may be unrelated in-progress work. Let me trace the run merge logic in EventStreamStateManager.

```text
625	 *
626	 * The changed-run delta is not optional politeness: the sidebar sorts every
627	 * project by `runs.lastActivityAt`, so a frame that carries only the selected
628	 * run freezes every other row's sort key until the next complete validation
629	 * poll a minute later. The list then reshuffles in bursts, and whichever
630	 * conversation the user opens is the only one with a current key — so it always
631	 * appears newest. Sending the rows that actually changed keeps ordering live
632	 * without putting the whole catalog on every frame.
633	 *
634	 * Partial frames deliberately omit the complete-catalog checksum. Otherwise
635	 * a change to an unselected run could advance the client's checksum without
```

```text
1	import type { runs } from "@/server/db/schema";
2	
3	type RunRecord = typeof runs.$inferSelect;
4	
5	/**
6	 * Trim run rows before they go on the wire.
7	 *
8	 * `db.select().from(runs)` pulls every column, including large JSON blobs the
9	 * client never reads. Measured on a real database: 130 non-archived runs
10	 * carried 142 KB of `gitBaselineJson` alone, and that rode along in *every*
11	 * snapshot frame for *every* connected client. Nothing in `src/components` or
12	 * `src/interface` reads it — the field exists only as a declaration in
13	 * `shared/home-types.ts`.
14	 *
15	 * Rules encoded here:
16	 * - `gitBaselineJson` / `plannerReadinessVerdictJson`: no frontend consumer at
17	 *   all, dropped outright.
18	 * - `plannerArtifactsJson`: read only for the selected run
19	 *   (`ConversationMain.tsx` → `PlanningArtifactsPanel`), so only the selected
20	 *   run pays for it.
21	 * - `gitWorkspaceJson`: read per run by `RunWorkspaceBadge`, kept for all.
22	 *
23	 * If a new surface needs one of the dropped fields, fetch it from a run-scoped
24	 * endpoint rather than re-broadening the broadcast payload — see Rule 2 in
25	 * `docs/architecture/hot-path-responsiveness-and-resource-leaks.md`.
26	 */
27	export function stripUnusedRunSnapshotFields<T extends RunRecord>(
28	  run: T,
29	  options: { selectedRunId?: string | null } = {},
30	) {
31	  const {
32	    gitBaselineJson: _gitBaselineJson,
33	    plannerReadinessVerdictJson: _plannerReadinessVerdictJson,
34	    plannerArtifactsJson,
35	    ...rest
36	  } = run;
37	
38	  return {
39	    ...rest,
40	    plannerArtifactsJson: run.id === options.selectedRunId ? plannerArtifactsJson : null,
41	  };
42	}
43
```

```text
100	function mergeReadMarkersForVisibleRuns(current: EventStreamState, incoming: EventStreamState) {
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:37:04.018Z

I'm checking mergeByKey's ordering behavior—since sorting happens by timestamp in buildConversationGroups, array order shouldn't matter unless there's a tie, which would then be resolved by id. So the actual flip must come from a value difference, possibly lastActivityAt differing between frames for the same run.

I'm also considering whether the non-authoritative merge path in mergeScopedRuns keeps the current run intact when it's newer, which seems fine. That leads me to wonder whether useFrozenRecentOrder only applies to the Active tab rather than Projects—I need to check how HomeApp.tsx passes activeProjects and filteredProjects around that area.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:37:09.490Z

The Projects tab actually sorts live by the most recent activity or creation time, so that part's working as intended—sessions move up when active. But I'm noticing one session oscillating up and down between two neighbors repeatedly, which points to a value flickering rather than a sort bug. I suspect partial SSE update frames and full snapshot frames are disagreeing on the timestamp, possibly in how the non-authoritative merge branch handles run update times.

I want to check whether the timestamp field being used only reflects `updatedAt` and ignores `lastActivityAt`, which could mean a locally-fresher run gets overwritten by a stale incoming row, causing the flip-flop. I'll look at lines 1-90 and 460-596 to trace this.

```text
10	function messageTimestampMs(message: MessageRecord) {
11	  const value = new Date(message.createdAt).getTime();
12	  return Number.isFinite(value) ? value : 0;
13	}
14	
15	function sortMessages(messages: MessageRecord[]) {
16	  return [...messages].sort((a, b) => {
17	    const timeDelta = messageTimestampMs(a) - messageTimestampMs(b);
18	    return timeDelta !== 0 ? timeDelta : a.id.localeCompare(b.id);
19	  });

22	type RunRecord = EventStreamState["runs"][number];

24	/**
25	 * True when a rebuilt state carries nothing the current one does not already
26	 * have, so the incoming copy can be dropped and the existing object identity
27	 * kept.
28	 *
29	 * The server stamps every snapshot with `snapshotChecksum` over the whole
30	 * payload (`server/events/payload-checksum.ts`), so an equal checksum means an
31	 * identical server-side view. Comparing the merged arrays by identity would be
32	 * useless here — a re-delivered payload is a fresh `JSON.parse`, so every array
33	 * is a new object even when byte-identical. The checksum is the only cheap
34	 * signal that actually distinguishes "same data" from "new data".
35	 *
36	 * Scope-defining fields are still compared, because a frame that only switches
37	 * `snapshotRunId` must not be swallowed just because the catalog hashed the
38	 * same.
39	 *
40	 * Dropping the duplicate also protects optimistic state: after a local
41	 * mutation, `snapshotChecksum` still reflects the last *server* view, so a poll
42	 * that re-delivers that same view would otherwise revert the optimistic change
43	 * and flicker until the server caught up.
44	 */
45	function isNoOpSnapshotUpdate(current: EventStreamState, next: EventStreamState) {
46	  // A partial frame intentionally retains the last complete checksum. Its
47	  // selected-run records may still have changed and must be merged.
48	  if (next.snapshotScope?.catalog?.complete === false) {
49	    return false;
50	  }
51	  const checksum = next.snapshotChecksum;
52	  if (!checksum || checksum !== current.snapshotChecksum) {
53	    return false;
54	  }
55	
56	  return next.snapshotRunId === current.snapshotRunId
57	    && next.snapshotSource === current.snapshotSource
58	    && next.messageScope?.complete === current.messageScope?.complete;
59	}
60	
61	function mergeByKey<T>(current: T[] | undefined, incoming: T[] | undefined, getKey: (item: T) => string | null | undefined) {
62	  const incomingItems = incoming ?? [];
63	  const seen = new Set<string>();
64	  const merged: T[] = [];
65	
66	  for (const item of incomingItems) {
67	    const key = getKey(item);
68	    if (key) {
69	      seen.add(key);
70	    }
71	    merged.push(item);
72	  }
73	
74	  for (const item of current ?? []) {
75	    const key = getKey(item);
76	    if (!key || seen.has(key)) {
77	      continue;
78	    }
79	    seen.add(key);
80	    merged.push(item);
81	  }
82	
83	  return merged;
84	}
85	
86	function runUpdatedTimestampMs(run: RunRecord) {
87	  const value = new Date(run.updatedAt || run.createdAt).getTime();
88	  return Number.isFinite(value) ? value : 0;
89	}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:37:29.682Z

I should actually verify this rather than speculate — I'll check the database for runs matching that session prefix and look at their lastActivityAt timestamps to see if the ordering is ambiguous, like duplicate timestamps or a race where two code paths update lastActivityAt inconsistently for the same run.

Actually, another suspicion: the timestamp might be serialized inconsistently — sometimes as an epoch number, sometimes as a string. If a numeric epoch arrives as a string, parsing it with `new Date()` could produce an Invalid Date and drop the run's ordering to zero, causing it to oscillate between two different serialization paths that don't handle this the same way.

I should verify by checking the local database directly and querying the run ordering and event snapshots for this run id, since the real culprit is likely that the underlying `lastActivityAt` value itself changes between frames — partial update frames may only include changed runs, leaving unchanged ones with stale values that flip the sort key back and forth.

I'm now looking at `mergeScopedRuns`'s non-authoritative branch, which fully replaces incoming run fields with the current cached run's fields — this is where client-side cache updates could clobber `lastActivityAt`. Optimistic local updates in useHomeMutations might construct run objects without `lastActivityAt`, causing the sort key to fall back to the older `createdAt`, which would explain the run dropping down and then popping back up once a server frame restores the correct value — repeating this on every mutation or poll would produce the oscillation. I want to grep for all the client-side spots that construct run objects, and there's a specific candidate around the `workerOutputObservedAtByRunId` tracking that's worth checking next.

Since `sidebarWorkerActivityManager` only touches the Active tab, that's not the source — so I'll grep useHomeMutations for how it maps over runs.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:37:52.978Z

I'm looking at target 86047b0864e7's position between 9d8d57be2c9e and af02ec56b735, with a sort key of 1790033453 sitting in a narrow band between neighbor keys 1790033426 and 1790033701 — if the computed key drifts outside that range, it would swap places with a neighbor.

My hypothesis is that the sort key sometimes uses `updatedAt` instead of `lastActivityAt`, and when 86047's `lastActivityAt` intermittently comes through as null (falling back to `createdAt`), it drops below af02ec56b735's key, then jumps back up when restored — matching the observed up-and-down movement. I'm checking whether `lastActivityAt` could get dropped somewhere in the payload path, looking at persisted-snapshot.ts and the events.ts route, both of which seem to serialize it correctly, so now I'm wondering if the issue is in how partial SSE frames handle `changedRunIds` deltas.

Actually, since the running run 9d8d57be2c9e only updates its `lastActivityAt` on turns, that's probably not oscillating on its own — so maybe the Projects tab sidebar applies its own separate sort or grouping logic rather than relying directly on the shared key. I want to check ConversationSidebar's rendering code, particularly whether it sorts by `updatedAt` or groups active runs first, around lines 280-430.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:38:34.230Z

I'm tracing who calls applyServerEventStreamState—both the LiveEventConnectionManager's SSE frames and the validation poll feed into it, so I need to check how a scoped partial catalog update merges when the client is watching a different, still-running session.

Complete frames replace the run rows wholesale with server data, and cache hydration on session switch also preserves the current state correctly, so that's not the culprit either. Maybe I should stop reasoning statically and instead check whether there's a prior fix for this exact flapping/reshuffling symptom in the commit history or docs, or look more closely at how changedRunIds gets computed in the partial SSE frame to see if stale run entries could sneak through.

Following the merge logic further, incoming runs get placed first and the remainder appended, so ordering shifts before the final sort corrects it — that part seems fine. Plans referenced by conversation groups are also preserved correctly across partial frames since merging retains any plans not included in the update.

Thinking about the actual symptom again — a session swapping positions with its neighbor repeatedly — I'm now looking closely at whether the neighboring running session's sort key alternates between different timestamp fields, which would explain the back-and-forth reordering.

I wonder if state.runs might have duplicate entries for the same id, though that would show visible duplicate rows rather than reordering. It might be more productive to check the server's SSE payload directly, seeing if there's a local API or running process I can query empirically.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:38:47.872Z

Since there's no re-sorting, the order should just follow whatever buildConversationGroups outputs. I want to trace how changedRunIds gets computed and check whether shareInFlightEventPayload might be caching a stale run row keyed by selectedRunId from an earlier point in time.

A stale cached payload could deliver an older run row that a server-authoritative merge accepts unconditionally, since mergeScopedRuns has no monotonic timestamp guard for server frames—unlike the non-authoritative branch which checks runUpdatedTimestampMs. That would explain the row regressing and then bouncing back once a fresh frame arrives, so I need to look at how the payload cache and changedRunIds are computed inside the SSE loop to find which server frames could actually be stale.

```text
715	  cache: Map<string, EventPayloadCacheEntry>,

717	  build: () => Promise<EventPayload>,

719	  const key = eventPayloadCacheKey(options);
720	  const version = getEventStreamNotificationVersion();
721	  const existing = cache.get(key);
722	  if (existing?.version === version) {
723	    return existing.promise;
724	  }
725	
726	  const pending = build().catch((error) => {
727	    const cached = cache.get(key);
728	    if (cached?.promise === pending) {
729	      cache.delete(key);
730	    }
731	    throw error;
732	  });
733	  cache.set(key, { version, promise: pending });
734	  pruneEventPayloadCache(cache);

736	}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:39:11.919Z

Found a strong candidate: the code sends the persisted payload update, then awaits the runtime payload promise afterward. Since both are built from DB records around the same time, the underlying run rows should match, but I notice the persisted and runtime payloads are constructed using different builder functions, which could explain a discrepancy.

I'm checking whether both paths ultimately funnel through the same field-stripping logic, and whether date serialization differs between the persisted-snapshot module and the events module — both seem to produce ISO strings consistently, so that's likely not the culprit. I'm now wondering if there's a separate stream involved, like the runner/bridge lock file or the VS Code extension, that could be introducing the mismatch, and I want to step back and check the client side next.

Now I'm testing whether the ordering flip could come from timestamp handling on the client — specifically whether `lastActivityAt` might sometimes arrive as a raw epoch-seconds number instead of being converted by Drizzle, which would break sorting badly rather than shift one slot. I'm working out what specific timestamp value would explain the run moving exactly one position, and I'm zeroing in on the idea that falling back to `createdAt` instead of `lastActivityAt` for that particular run would produce exactly the observed shift.

The pattern of moving down one slot then back up matches a run whose `lastActivityAt` is intermittently missing on the client, causing it to alternate between using `lastActivityAt` and falling back to `createdAt`. Now I want to search the codebase for places constructing a run object with an explicit field list that might omit `lastActivityAt`, like optimistic conversation creation paths.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:39:43.345Z

Since auth is blocking that path, I should reason empirically instead about which frames the client actually gets for a run in a different project. Rereading `buildEventStreamPayload`, partial frames only include selected and changed runs, and on the client, `mergeScopedCatalog` checks `catalogIsPartial` — when true it routes into the merge branch rather than replacing the catalog wholesale.

If the snapshot is complete, the runs just get replaced outright with the full catalog, which is straightforward. But if no session is selected, the payload stays unchanged as a complete catalog, meaning ordering is purely DB-derived and stable — so the user must have a session open for this partial-frame behavior to matter, since partial frames mark the catalog incomplete and only include the selected run's ID as complete, omitting the checksum so the client keeps its current one. The merge logic puts incoming rows first, then appends existing rows not present in incoming, which reorders the array — and since sorting downstream is supposed to be total, this should be fine unless something else is going on.

I'm second-guessing whether `buildConversationGroups`'s memoization or the search/filter logic could be involved, but those seem fine. Maybe the reordering the user sees is actually a React key-instability issue — index-based keys causing rows to remount — though that doesn't quite match "moving up and down." I should check the sidebar tests for anything about reshuffling or ordering, or look for a known-issue doc describing this exact behavior.

Now I'm wondering if resolveStoredProjectRoot could be unstable if it depends on the ordering of explicitProjects, which itself could get reordered — that could cause a run to flip between two project groups entirely, which would look like it's jumping up and down the session list.

Actually I should stop speculating and think about how to reproduce this deterministically rather than guess blindly — though a stable, monotone sort would be a safe fix regardless of root cause. Let me reconsider whether there are two concurrent server sources fighting each other: an SSE stream sending partial frames scoped to the selected run, plus a periodic full snapshot poll every few seconds that might also be scoped by runId — that combination could explain the flicker.

I want to check whether the partial frames are truly built from live DB data every time, since if `buildEventPayload` pulls `records.allRuns` fresh each time there shouldn't be any regression — unless `readPersistedEventRecords` is caching a stale snapshot for non-selected runs. I need to look at that function, particularly around line 121, to see if it's holding onto cached data for runs that aren't currently selected.

```text
130	        selectedRunId,
131	      ]
132	    : [];

134	  const [
135	    msgs,
136	    allAccounts,
137	    allWorkers,
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:40:37.077Z

The grouping path looks stable. Let me reconsider whether this database row is static — the run status is "done" with a last_activity_at timestamp that converts to just minutes ago, so this is very recent activity, not something stale.

Looking at neighboring rows, I spot a real mechanism: for a running session, lastActivityAt only updates when a worker turn ends or a user sends a message, via a trigger on status change out of "working." During a long turn a worker can flip between working and idle repeatedly, and each flip bumps lastActivityAt to now — so a running session's sort key jumps upward periodically, always monotonically increasing, never oscillating.

But that still can't explain 86047 moving down, since once another run passes it above, they can't move back below — both partial frames and full snapshot refreshes only push values forward. I start wondering if the tab logic itself is the culprit: when the active tab is "projects," `useFrozenRecentOrder` returns the list unfrozen, and I notice both the projects and recent groupings seem to be rendered simultaneously in the markup, just possibly hidden by CSS rather than conditionally mounted — which could mean the wrong list is being displayed under certain conditions.

But I need to check what `stableActiveProjects` actually feeds into, since HomeApp seems to pass it separately from `filteredProjects`. I should trace how the "Active" section at the top of the Projects tab is populated versus the full sorted list below it, since the user could be seeing behavior from the Active section rather than the main sorted list.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:41:01.707Z

I'm tracing whether the snapshot polling endpoint, which requests persisted data scoped to a specific run, actually returns the complete catalog or just a partial payload via the event stream builder — checking how handleEventsRequest's snapshot branch constructs its response.

If it's the complete catalog, the client just replaces all runs with fresh DB rows, which seems fine. The real mystery is what causes a run row to regress on the client, so I'm digging into applyUpdate and isNotModifiedSnapshot, then the SSE stream's change-detection logic that tracks delivered run payloads by serializing each run and comparing against what was previously sent to determine which runs actually changed. I'm also noting that stripUnusedRunSnapshotFields nulls out planner artifacts for non-selected runs consistently per connection, but the payload alternates between a persisted version and a runtime-enriched version when the bridge is slow — so I need to check whether these two variants produce differing run row representations that could cause the discrepancy.

Since the runtime-enriched payload re-reads records after a bridge sync that can update run status or timestamps, the two payload variants can legitimately differ, but they should still be monotone in lastActivityAt, so that's likely not the bug. I'm running out of promising leads from pure code reading — considering whether this could be a duplicate run id causing a rendering glitch, but that feels unlikely, so I'm leaning toward writing an actual reproduction test using the real client merge and group-builder logic.

I don't have the actual event frames to reproduce this with, and instrumenting the live app would mean adding a dev-only log that changes the workspace, which isn't really appropriate for a fix. Let me instead go back and look more carefully at `applyUpdate` in LiveEventConnectionManager to see if it's merging runs with a partial patch in a way that could explain this.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:41:44.908Z

The DB order is stable, so this reordering must be happening client-side. The item at position #2 (86047) sits between #1 (9d8d) and #3 (af02) and occasionally swaps with one of them — matching the "surrounded by 2 other ones" pattern exactly. Looking at their timestamps, 86047 and af02 have sort keys only 27 seconds apart, which could explain the flickering swap.

I'm working through two possible scenarios: if 86047 loses its lastActivityAt and falls back to createdAt, it would drop below af02 but stay above the next item, moving down one slot and then back up — matching the symptom perfectly. Alternatively, if 9d8d loses its lastActivityAt instead, it would drop below both 86047 and af02, causing 86047 to briefly become #1. Both variants require some client row to temporarily lose its lastActivityAt field, so I'm now trying to figure out where in the client code a runs array entry could end up missing that field — possibly during cache hydration, the initial bootstrap payload embedded in the HTML, or some fallback path in the state synchronization logic.

I'm now wondering whether there's a second source of runs data, like a runtime API list call used by some query, that gets merged into state and could introduce a stale row missing lastActivityAt. I should grep for that API and any client code calling it.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:41:57.719Z

With no runs.list available, the event payloads must be the only source of truth, so I need to trace how the client's merge logic handles a partial frame — filtering out absent completed runs from the current set, then merging in incoming runs by id, which preserves all current rows.

When the frame is complete, the code just returns the incoming data wholesale with updated read markers, which should be fresh from the database — so that's not the culprit either. What catches my eye now is the possibility of a stale closure: if a mutation handler calls `setState` using a captured, non-functional `state` object rather than the functional updater form, it could silently revert the runs array to an older snapshot, causing the visible regression until the next server frame corrects it. ## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:42:32.523Z

I notice something about `deliveredRunPayloads` being per-connection, updated only when a frame actually sends — on the very first frame, it's null, so changedRunIds is empty and the frame carries just the selected run, since the client already has the full catalog from the snapshot poll. That's fine for bootstrap, but now I need to think through what happens on a reconnect instead.

With EventSource auto-reconnect, a fresh connection resets `deliveredRunPayloads` to null again, so the new first frame is selected-run-only, incomplete, and the client just merges it in and keeps old rows — nothing regresses. So partial frames can only omit data, never roll it backward, unless the client's `current.runs` somehow gets replaced by something stale. I'm digging into `mergeScopedCatalog`, tracing how it computes absent complete run IDs and filters current runs against them — if the selected run isn't in the incoming frame, it gets dropped client-side. Still nothing conclusive, so I'm shifting to examine the sidebar unread/active hints instead.

I'm now reconsidering whether the "moving up and down" effect is really the whole project group shifting rather than an individual row, but since the user said it's flanked by two others, it seems to be within-group movement. Then I wonder if the row is actually jumping between the project group and an "Other sessions" bucket — which would look exactly like a session moving up and down the list. I'm checking whether `run.projectPath` staying set keeps the group path stable regardless of plan data, and tracing through `stripUnusedRunSnapshotFields` and `resolveStoredProjectRoot`/`resolveStaleProjectFallback` for any instability that could cause that regrouping.

Since `resolveStaleProjectFallback` is derived from the full set of runs' projectPaths in the current state and applied to all runs, its output could shift as the runs array itself changes over time, so I'm digging into how it's implemented.

```text
1	export function normalizeProjectPath(projectPath: string) {
2	  return projectPath.trim().replace(/[/\\]+$/, "") || "/";
3	}
4	
5	function pathSeparatorFor(projectPath: string) {
6	  return projectPath.includes("\\") && !projectPath.includes("/") ? "\\" : "/";
7	}
8	
9	export function isSameOrDescendantPath(pathValue: string, rootValue: string) {
10	  const path = normalizeProjectPath(pathValue);
11	  const root = normalizeProjectPath(rootValue);
12	  if (path === root) {
13	    return true;
14	  }
15	
16	  return path.startsWith(`${root}${pathSeparatorFor(root)}`);
17	}
18	
19	export function normalizeExplicitProjectPaths(explicitProjects: string[]) {
20	  return Array.from(new Set(
21	    explicitProjects
22	      .map((projectPath) => normalizeProjectPath(projectPath))
23	      .filter((projectPath) => projectPath.length > 0),
24	  ));
25	}
26	
27	export function resolveStaleProjectFallback(
28	  explicitProjects: string[],
29	  storedProjectPaths: Array<string | null | undefined>,
30	) {
31	  const normalizedExplicitProjects = normalizeExplicitProjectPaths(explicitProjects);
32	  if (normalizedExplicitProjects.length === 0) {
33	    return null;
34	  }
35	
36	  const directRunCounts = new Map(normalizedExplicitProjects.map((projectPath) => [projectPath, 0]));
37	  let hasUnmatchedStoredPath = false;
38	
39	  for (const storedProjectPath of storedProjectPaths) {
40	    const storedPath = storedProjectPath?.trim()
41	      ? normalizeProjectPath(storedProjectPath)
42	      : null;
43	    if (!storedPath) {
44	      continue;
45	    }
46	
47	    const matchingExplicitProject = normalizedExplicitProjects.find((projectPath) => (
48	      isSameOrDescendantPath(storedPath, projectPath)
49	    ));
50	
51	    if (matchingExplicitProject) {
52	      directRunCounts.set(matchingExplicitProject, (directRunCounts.get(matchingExplicitProject) ?? 0) + 1);
53	    } else {
54	      hasUnmatchedStoredPath = true;
55	    }
56	  }
57	
58	  if (!hasUnmatchedStoredPath) {
59	    return null;
60	  }
61	
62	  const emptyExplicitProjects = normalizedExplicitProjects.filter((projectPath) => (
63	    (directRunCounts.get(projectPath) ?? 0) === 0
64	  ));
65	
66	  return emptyExplicitProjects.length === 1 ? emptyExplicitProjects[0] : null;
67	}
68	
69	export function resolveStoredProjectRoot(
70	  storedProjectPath: string | null | undefined,
71	  explicitProjects: string[],
72	  options: { staleFallbackProject?: string | null } = {},
73	) {
74	  const normalizedExplicitProjects = normalizeExplicitProjectPaths(explicitProjects);
75	  const storedPath = storedProjectPath?.trim()
76	    ? normalizeProjectPath(storedProjectPath)
77	    : null;
78	
79	  if (!storedPath) {
80	    return null;
81	  }
82	
83	  const matchingExplicitProject = normalizedExplicitProjects.find((projectPath) => (
84	    isSameOrDescendantPath(storedPath, projectPath)
85	  ));
86	  if (matchingExplicitProject) {
87	    return matchingExplicitProject;
88	  }
89	
90	  if (normalizedExplicitProjects.length === 0) {
91	    return storedPath;
92	  }
93	
94	  if (normalizedExplicitProjects.length === 1) {
95	    return normalizedExplicitProjects[0];
96	  }
97	
98	  if (options.staleFallbackProject) {
99	    return normalizeProjectPath(options.staleFallbackProject);
100	  }
101	
102	  return null;
103	}
104
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:43:40.191Z

Three sessions in the same project are all active within minutes of each other, and each turn completion bumps lastActivityAt, pushing that session's row to the top while the others get displaced. That's the reshuffling behavior the user is seeing, and it's expected since parallel sessions are the app's normal mode.

The fix is to stop the Projects tab from live-reshuffling, the way the Recent tab already does with its `useFrozenRecentOrder` hook. Since project group order is already user-defined and stable (draggable), only the run order within each group needs freezing — I can lock that order while the tab is visible, re-sorting on re-entry, while still inserting new runs and dropping deleted ones. This means extending the existing hook or building a new `useStableProjectOrder` hook following the same pattern.

New rows not yet in the snapshot get placed at the front in live order, then merged into the stored snapshot so they stay put in future frames rather than shuffling. I still need to figure out when to reset the freeze (leaving the tab, remount) and confirm filtering/collapsed groups don't complicate the ordering, since frozen order within a collapsed group is harmless.

Group order itself comes from the user-defined project ordering and is already stable, so freezing only matters for run order within a group. I'm now weighing whether freezing is even the right approach versus just sorting by createdAt, which never changes and would be inherently stable for a "Projects" browse list.

Sorting by createdAt is simpler but changes the useful behavior of surfacing recently-worked-on sessions at the top, which the existing code comment implies is intentional. I'd rather preserve that "recency on re-entry, no live reshuffle" property by mirroring the Recent tab's approach, so I'm planning to check how HomeApp wires up active/stable projects and generalize the existing frozen-order hook into a reusable stable sidebar order hook.

Now I need to wire this into the Projects tab similarly, pass the stabilized list into ConversationSidebar's props, and check the existing tests for the frozen-order hook to add coverage for the new case.

Vanishing sessions disappear, project membership and ordering stay live from user settings, and switching away from or reloading the Projects tab re-sorts everything by current activity — so the goal is to freeze only the run order within a group while keeping group membership dynamic. This is getting complicated, so I'm stepping back to think about actual scenarios: a new conversation appearing should land at the top, a run moved into a project likely goes to the top too, but when a search filter clears and many runs become newly visible at once, freezing survivors in place while inserting new ones sensibly is trickier — really that case probably calls for a different resolution entirely.

I'll settle on a simple, explainable rule: runs not present in the frozen order go to the front of their group in live order, survivors keep their frozen positions — reasoning that a session absent when the order froze is exactly where activity-based sorting would naturally place it anyway. That's imperfect for a cleared search filter, since newly unfrozen rows would bunch at the top, but I'm considering whether a search query change should just trigger a fresh snapshot since it's a deliberate user action that justifies re-sorting.

But then I realize freezing-on-mount is risky since the Projects tab is the default, left open for hours — a snapshot taken once would never refresh, letting a session used hours ago sit above one used minutes ago. That staleness makes me reconsider the freeze approach.

I think re-snapshotting when the sidebar remounts, search changes, or the tab is re-entered mitigates this without reintroducing jitter — selection changes shouldn't trigger reordering though. Still weighing whether a more surgical approach exists that keeps freshness while killing jitter entirely.

I'll settle on freeze-on-view plus documented reset conditions (tab switch, search change, reload), which directly addresses the reported symptom, and confirm the activity-based sort itself is intentional for the Projects browse list rather than something to change. Now I want to verify that even with frozen ordering, individual rows don't still cause visual churn on re-render.

Group order for Projects doesn't need freezing since it's already stable — explicit project order with "other" appended last. I'm keeping the live list feeding into the fallback row-data source since order isn't the concern there, then checking whether filteredProjects has other usages in the component before adding tests for the new hook.

```json
{
```

```json
  "file_path": "/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useFrozenRecentOrder.ts",
  "old_string": "export function useFrozenRecentOrder(\n  activeProjects: SidebarGroup[],\n  isRecentTabActive: boolean,\n  liveCatalogProjects: SidebarGroup[] = activeProjects,\n): SidebarGroup[] {",
  "new_string": "/**\n * Same problem, different tab. The Projects tab sorts every project's sessions\n * by `lastActivityAt`, which the server advances each time a worker finishes a\n * turn (`runs_activity_*` triggers). Run two or three sessions in one project —\n * the normal way this app is used — and their rows trade places every few\n * seconds, so the session the user is reaching for walks up and down the list.\n *\n * Membership here stays live, unlike the Recent tab: a conversation created\n * while the tab is open must show up, and a deleted one must go. Only the\n * relative order of the sessions already on screen is pinned, and it is\n * recaptured when the tab is re-entered, when the search query changes, and on\n * reload. Rows keep updating in place, so status, titles and unread dots are\n * never stale — just the row's position is.\n */\nexport function useStableProjectOrder(\n  projects: SidebarGroup[],\n  isProjectsTabActive: boolean,\n  resetKey = \"\",\n): SidebarGroup[] {\n  const runOrderRef = useRef<Map<string, string[]> | null>(null);\n  const resetKeyRef = useRef(resetKey);\n\n  if (!isProjectsTabActive) {\n    // Tab hidden — drop the pinned order so re-entry sorts by latest activity.\n    runOrderRef.current = null;\n    resetKeyRef.current = resetKey;\n    return projects;\n  }\n\n  if (resetKeyRef.current !== resetKey) {\n    runOrderRef.current = null;\n    resetKeyRef.current = resetKey;\n  }\n\n  const frozenRunOrder = runOrderRef.current;\n  const nextRunOrder = new Map<string, string[]>();\n  const stabilized = projects.map((project) => {\n    const frozenOrder = frozenRunOrder?.get(project.path);\n    const runs = frozenOrder ? applyRunOrder(project.runs, frozenOrder) : project.runs;\n    nextRunOrder.set(project.path, runs.map((run) => run.id));\n    return runs === project.runs ? project : { ...project, runs };\n  });\n\n  runOrderRef.current = nextRunOrder;\n  return stabilized;\n}\n\n/**\n * Sessions already in the pinned order keep it; anything that arrived since —\n * a new conversation, one moved into this project, one the search query just\n * revealed — enters at the top, which is where the activity sort would have\n * put it anyway.\n */\nfunction applyRunOrder(liveRuns: SnapshottedRun[], frozenOrder: string[]): SnapshottedRun[] {\n  const liveRunsById = new Map(liveRuns.map((run) => [run.id, run]));\n  const frozenRunIds = new Set(frozenOrder);\n  const entered = liveRuns.filter((run) => !frozenRunIds.has(run.id));\n  const retained = frozenOrder.flatMap((runId) => {\n    const run = liveRunsById.get(runId);\n    return run ? [run] : [];\n  });\n  const ordered = [...entered, ...retained];\n\n  // Nothing actually moved — hand back the live array so memoized consumers\n  // downstream (the sidebar's content key, the row list) keep their identity.\n  const unchanged = ordered.length === liveRuns.length\n    && ordered.every((run, index) => run.id === liveRuns[index]?.id);\n  return unchanged ? liveRuns : ordered;\n}\n\nexport function useFrozenRecentOrder(\n  activeProjects: SidebarGroup[],\n  isRecentTabActive: boolean,\n  liveCatalogProjects: SidebarGroup[] = activeProjects,\n): SidebarGroup[] {"
}
```

## Claude · 2026-09-21T23:46:19.314Z

The row wasn't jumping because of a broken state merge — the data is correct and the sort is deterministic. The Projects tab just sorted live.

What's happening with your three dis3 sessions:

| session | last activity |
|---|---|
| `9d8d57be2c9e` (running) | 23:38:20 |
| `86047b0864e7` | 23:30:53 |
| `af02ec56b735` | 23:30:26 |

`runs.last_activity_at` is bumped by the `runs_activity_on_worker_turn_end` / `on_user_message` triggers (`src/server/db/index.ts:973`), and the Projects list sorts on `max(lastActivityAt, createdAt)` on every event-stream frame (`src/lib/conversations.ts:50`). All three of those sessions were working in the same project within the same few minutes, so each turn end hoisted one row to the top and pushed `86047b0864e7` down a slot — then its own next turn end pulled it back up. Between the same two neighbours, over and over.

The Recent tab already had a fix for exactly this (`useFrozenRecentOrder`, added with the comment "rows can disappear, appear, or reshuffle on every turn"). The Projects tab never got one.

Changes:

- `src/interface/home/useFrozenRecentOrder.ts` — new `useStableProjectOrder` hook. While the Projects tab is open it pins the relative order of the sessions on screen, per project. Unlike the Recent tab's freeze, membership stays live: a conversation created while you watch appears (at the top of its project, where the activity sort would have put it), and a deleted or archived one goes. Rows themselves still update in place, so status, titles and unread dots are current — only the position is held. The order is recaptured on tab re-entry, on a search-query change, and on reload.
- `src/interface/home/HomeApp.tsx:632` — wires the Projects tab through it, next to the existing Recent-tab freeze.
- `tests/app/use-stable-project-order.test.ts` — covers the overtake case (sibling finishes a turn, watched row stays put), live row refresh, new sessions appearing, removal, and both reset paths.

`tests/app` (574 tests), `tests/ui/sidebar-layout.test.ts`, `tests/lib/conversations.test.ts`, `tsc --noEmit` and eslint all pass; the one eslint warning in `HomeApp.tsx` is pre-existing and unrelated.

One tradeoff worth knowing: leave the Projects tab open for hours and its order reflects when you opened it, not the last five minutes. Switching to Recent and back re-sorts. If you'd rather it stay live-sorted and have the jitter solved another way — say, only letting a row move when it becomes the most recent in its project — that's a different rule and I can swap it in.

parent: aeca7ad3-b25a-4773-9e27-ac2333dceebe

```text
export const handleEventsRequest: OmniHttpHandler = async (request, context) => {
  const url = new URL(request.url);
  const isSnapshot = url.searchParams.get("snapshot") === "1";
  const persistedOnly = url.searchParams.get("persisted") === "1";
  const probe = isSnapshot
    ? startSlowProbe(`GET /api/events?snapshot=1${persistedOnly ? "&persisted=1" : ""}${url.searchParams.get("runId") ? `&runId=${url.searchParams.get("runId")}` : ""}`)
    : null;
  const ticketAuth = isSnapshot
    ? null
    : await redeemStreamTicketRequest(request, "/api/events");
  if (ticketAuth?.response) {
    probe?.end();
    return ticketAuth.response;
  }
  const auth = ticketAuth
    ? { session: null, response: null }
    : await requireApiSession(request, {
      source: "Events",
      action: "Stream live updates",
    });
  probe?.mark("auth");
  if (auth.response) {
    probe?.end();
    return auth.response;
  }

  const eventPayloadOptions = {
    selectedRunId: url.searchParams.get("runId"),
  } satisfies EventPayloadOptions;

  if (isSnapshot) {
    // Capture the transport boundary before reading SQLite/runtime state.
    // Events emitted while the snapshot is being assembled are therefore
    // replayed after this anchor. The snapshot may already contain their
    // resulting revision, but revision/event-key merges make that harmless;
    // anchoring after the build would silently skip an event the body missed.
    const snapshotAnchor = getEventStreamCursor();
    const payload = persistedOnly
      // `persisted=1` is the canonical verification surface used after
      // lifecycle mutations. It must reflect SQLite now, even when a route
      // bundle did not share the in-process notification version.
      ? await buildPersistedEventPayload(eventPayloadOptions, probe ?? undefined)
      : await (async () => {
        const { ensureSupervisorRuntimeStarted } = await import("@/server/supervisor/runtime-watchdog");
        await ensureSupervisorRuntimeStarted();
        probe?.mark("ensureSupervisor");
        return buildSharedRuntimeEnrichedEventPayload(eventPayloadOptions, probe ?? undefined);
      })();
    probe?.mark("payload.total");
    const requestedChecksum = url.searchParams.get("checksum")?.trim() || "";

    // Anchor the snapshot to the current cursor so a subsequent SSE
    // connection can pass `Last-Event-ID: <lastEventId>` (or
    // `?lastEventId=`) and receive only events newer than this body.
    // Exposed as a response header to avoid changing the JSON shape
    // that the UI consumes.
    const response = Response.json(
      requestedChecksum && requestedChecksum === payload.snapshotChecksum
        ? {
          notModified: true,
          snapshotChecksum: payload.snapshotChecksum,
          workerEntrySeqs: payload.workerEntrySeqs,
        }
        : payload,
    );
    response.headers.set("x-omni-last-event-id", snapshotAnchor);
    if (payload.snapshotChecksum) {
      response.headers.set("x-omni-snapshot-checksum", payload.snapshotChecksum);

    return response;

  const resumeFromCursorQuery = parseLastEventId(url.searchParams.get("cursor"));
  const resumeFromLegacyQuery = parseLastEventId(url.searchParams.get("lastEventId"));
  const resumeFromId = hasCursorQuery
    ? resumeFromCursorQuery
    : resumeFromHeader ?? resumeFromLegacyQuery;
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-21T23:55:52.564Z

Since sessions only store an HMAC, I can't recover an existing token, but I could mint a fresh one using the app's key file and a script that inserts a new auth session. That's reasonable for local debugging, though it does create new database state, so I'd rather avoid it if possible.

Instead I'm reasoning about whether I can reproduce the pipeline in-process against the same static database twice to compare outputs, but since the database doesn't change, the payloads would be identical and wouldn't expose a client-side or caching bug. So the real constraint is that the client-held value must diverge while the database stays static, and the client only ever gets rows from server payloads — which points me toward examining the non-authoritative merge path in `mergeScopedRuns`, checking whether incoming runs get overwritten by stale current runs based on their updated timestamps, and figuring out exactly when that path triggers versus the local update path.

Since this merge path is rare and the cache-hydration path also preserves current state rather than regressing it, I'm concluding the client genuinely can't roll back row values on its own — so the divergence has to come from the server sending different values at different times. That pushes me to reconstruct the actual timeline: correlating when a specific worker job finished against the UTC-to-local timezone offset to figure out exactly when the user was watching and complaining, and cross-referencing that against another job's `lastActivityAt` timestamps which were still advancing during that same window.

Now I'm reasoning through what would happen if 9d8d's `lastActivityAt` were only *sometimes* present on the client copy — dropping back to its `createdAt` value would push it below the other two jobs in sort order, and then a fresh frame with the field restored would push it back above them, producing the alternating "moving up and down" effect the user described for the second job.

I wonder if 9d8d, being the actively selected/streaming run, is somehow serialized differently on partial frames than the complete snapshot is. So far the `buildEventStreamPayload` logic looks identical between them, so I'm now considering whether named worker/run events on the client might be patching run rows separately and dropping fields in the process.
